ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3739	Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VG/XXI/M/M_00070/index.shtml -- 70/M]] Recent developments in the European agricultural market have been the result of years of suppressed problems, coupled with mass production aimed at reckless profit maximisation. In your view, what steps should be taken in the context of these developments in the EU's agricultural policy?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3766	Please have a reply, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3782	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I believe that by the 1992 reform, the European Union has set a first step towards direct payments for farmers, that Agenda 2000 has made a key decision that a second pillar of agricultural policy will finally be developed, which we have long been doing in Austria, now known under rural development. In this second pillar, there is the possibility of environmental programmes that Austria is most offensively using, mining promotion, of rural development within the meaning of cooperation projects à la 5b for the whole area. Nevertheless, I share your view that agricultural policy in Europe needs to develop further. And I would like to outline four major areas in which, in my view, it is necessary to make further progress. Firstly, in the less-favoured area. In all European countries, this is about making even greater use of the opportunities offered by the rural development programme, for example with regard to other offensives in environmental programmes or, for example, good agricultural practice. Secondly, I believe that in Europe there will be a reduction in direct payments, and market-regulation premiums in terms of operating sizes. This is, in particular, a key agricultural policy issue in the context of enlargement. Thirdly, I believe that we should strive to ensure that premiums are paid at European level. What do I mean? – That, for example, in the case of cattle premiums, the link between production and area must be given, because land-independent production is ultimately the basis for industrial production. I believe that we need uniform and high standards in the European Union, such as in the field of plant protection and veterinary medicinal products. I know that Mr Haupt is planning to do something like this, which I strongly support. And I also think that we should pay great attention to the principle of simplification, debureaucratisation. In my opinion, in the field of consumer protection, the Food Safety Agency is important, and in the field of consumer protection, the development of labelling and labelling, including the designation of origin, is crucial. The third major area is the issue of animal welfare. I assume that the Commission will soon present us with a proposal to improve animal transport conditions based on the experience of the control report. We are already intensively discussing a proposal to improve animal husbandry in the area of pig production. Fourthly, the question of the food strategy is a very central point also on the basis of the European Union White Paper on raising the quality and ensuring consumer safety requirements. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) You see, therefore, that from my point of view, the agricultural policy debate in Europe is comprehensive, including the aspects of consumer protection, ecological efforts and also the efforts of animal ethics.

There is a quota system for milk and sugar in the European agricultural market system in order to keep production volumes under control. Do you calculate that this quota system will continue to be maintained for milk and sugar in the future?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3925	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e3940	Deputies! You know that Austria is committed to maintaining this quota system. Why? – Because I think these production quotas have two effects: On the one hand, they have a damping effect on production itself and thus oppose a measure if you want to, overproduction. On the other hand – and this is important to me – they also have the effect that production is distributed to as many plants as possible. Without the milk quota system, for example, we would have a completely different production structure in Austria and Europe. Thirdly, the protection of production in less favoured areas is particularly important. Austria is therefore strongly committed to maintaining the quota. However, it is also true that other European countries have different views here. It will therefore be decisive that we have, on a broad basis, allies for our strategy for this 2002 review phase, 2003.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e4546	Mr. Federal Minister! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XXI/M/M_00066/index.shtml -- 66/M]] What are the key objectives to be achieved by establishing a Food Safety Agency?
ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e4574	Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e4589	Deputies! The aim of this Food Safety Agency is to: Firstly, effective and efficient control of food safety for the benefit of humans, animals and plants. Secondly, we want to create a pooling of enforcement and control capacity from the various legal areas, in a first stage of the legal areas of food law, veterinary law and of the whole of the business media law, i.e. feed law, plant protection law, fertiliser law and seed law. At a second stage of expansion, we want to invite the countries to support this agency and also to bring in their capacities so that we can ensure continuous control and effective implementation of all these legal areas. The aim of this Agency is therefore: Control from the field to the loading table, from the stable to the counter, so that this consistent control and efficiency principle represents safety and quality for consumers. We want to make efficient use of the laboratory capacities so that we can also take on additional and new control tasks. I also think that in the field of research and the coordination of research, this agency will also be able to take over a role in the field of food nutrition.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e5206	Mr. Federal Minister! In 1854, sustainability for the management of the Austrian forests was established in the ReichsForstgesetz. ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e5222	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e5237	Deputies! The water strategy already adopted by the Austrian Federal Government last year is one of the key elements in the concept of sustainability, and I stress: Sustainability means economic sustainability, environmental sustainability and social sustainability. The principle is therefore: Protecting is the first priority goal, and the second concerns responsible use. That's the concept. First of all, this means that we must keep water resources permanently usable, all of them: Flowwaters, lakes, but above all the groundwater. Secondly, this means maintaining the right to have all groundwater available as drinking water. In this we are clearly different from other European countries. Thirdly, we want to ensure the natural nature of our waters and, of course, to protect people from the dangers of water. I am thinking, for example, of flood protection construction or of the conversion measures in the mountain regions.

The first measure I see is that all the authorities, all the institutions, are doing all they can to identify, detect, suspect and sanction these criminal activities. Secondly, where it is necessary to increase efficiency, we need to improve the coordination of controls, such as with this food safety agency, as we are planning in Austria. Thirdly, we need to strengthen standards at European level – the ban on antibiotics. Fourth, we need to improve the legal basis in Austria – food law, veterinary medicinal products law. Fifthly, we must strive at European level for uniform enforcement and a uniform standard also in approval. Sixthly, I assume that in Austria it will be necessary to start something like marketing and image campaigns for responsible Austrian producers at the right time. My concern is that, because of criminal machinations, businesses that produce properly and responsibly are affected and, of course, also suffer economic damage. The crucial question, therefore, is that we quickly restore consumer confidence in the good Austrian products.

In principle, the Court of Auditors identified shortcomings in the award of services itself or as a result of the award of quality of the service register. But it is positive to note that the country of Lower Austria immediately became active to address these shortcomings. For example, an employee in the competent department was specifically assigned to deal intensively with the subject of awards. It has also installed its own training program for employees. The internal organization has also been changed. Corrigendum lists have been added to the list of services and so on. Many, many measures have been taken to address these shortcomings in the future. ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e15592	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Before addressing the specific questions addressed to me, I would like to draw your attention to some of the principles we take into account in the handling of demonstrations, and to show you the characteristics of the Austrian way in which it is now being taken into consideration throughout Europe – whole delegations come to us to study it in Vienna – namely the handling of freedom of demonstration in the sense of a red and white-red ministry and also the handling of the protection of the population and the institutions of the Republic of Austria entrusted to us. These characteristics and the principles can be combined in a sentence, that is, peaceful rallys, no blockades! Under this principle of law, we do our work. [[As a result of this, the Commission has decided to extend the scope of the agreement to the Member States of the European Union.]] We do this not only because it is fully in line with the Austrian legal order, but also because we do not want to see pictures about and from Austria, how we can see them every fourteen days from Berlin, Prague, Nice, Paris or wherever. We don't want bloody street battles, we don't want mass cramps, we want a climate of deescalation and an Austrian path. [[It is a pleasure to see the ÖVP and the liberals, as well as to the SPÖ representatives.]] Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The right to freedom of assembly is guaranteed by constitutional law in Austria, in the basic law of 1867 and in the 1958 European Convention on Human Rights, which was fully integrated into our constitution. The fundamental right of assembly as enshrined in the Austrian Constitution is to be fully respected as one of the highest democratic goods. Interventions in the fundamental right to assembly freedom are only possible in the framework drawn from the Convention on Human Rights and Assembly Law. This includes careful consideration of the affected and protected interests. Now to a number of issues, which – sometimes quite rightly and understandably – lead to discussions and concerns among some of the population groups. For example, the question: Why are unshown demonstrations not dissolved? The omission of the adjudication of a public assembly according to the case law of the Constitutional Court is not, on its own, yet the basis for a ban or disbandment of the assembly, but probably for the imposition of an administrative penalty. In order to prohibit or disband an unannounced assembly, there must always be additional circumstances, such as the danger of protective goods also mentioned in the Convention on Human Rights and the Convention on Human Rights. I would like to stress at this point that the competent authorities have made and made their decisions solely on the basis of the relevant legal situation, and that after a very careful consideration of interests. [[Demonstrative applause with the SPÖ and the Greens.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! All rallys have a right to protection if they are carried out peacefully and are duly notified. This means the peaceful rallying participants, including party organizers, and among other things concerned citizens who at our northern border benefit from their right of assembly to point to a situation threatening from their point of view in our northern neighboring country. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on the SPÖ and the Greens.]] At all these meetings, at all these gatherings, at all these events, the Spagat between the fundamental right to freedom of assembly and the safeguarding of the rights and interests of others, not affected – such as the protection of health and morality or the protection of rights and freedoms, as well as the public interest in national and public security, as well as the maintenance of order and crime prevention – is penable and accurate. We are going this Austrian path, which is summarized in four points: Happy, properly displayed congregations – yes! Violent demos or road blockades – no! That is, not to cut down on the right of demonstration, but to preserve the rights and interests of the people directly affected by the demonstrations, because they live and work alongside the place where these demonstrations are located. Thirdly, discussions by the Authority with event applicants should ensure that there is no total blockades that hinder unparticipated passers-by, people on the way to work, operational vehicles or others. Fourthly, there should be clear consequences if these basic rules are infringed: from the imposition of the administrative penalties required by law to the prohibition of a assembly necessary to protect the security, to protect the rights and freedoms of others in our democratic society, as happened in the individual case, for example in the so-called "Checkpoint Austria" action – we could discuss this here in the House on the same day – or also in the recently held place in Oberlaa. Summary: Let us go an Austrian path of proportionality and de-escalation, which means a balance of interests between freedom of assembly and the interests of other people! That's – and that's what's going on – a walk that I've been convinced so far, with my fingertips, with discipline and with consistent intervention where it was necessary, was also well mastered. It is a targeted, decisive intervention of executives in violence and outpouring. As with any intervention, the principle of proportionality applies. We are and do not want to be Rambos, but we will ensure that people in this country can look at the security device as a trusted institution. In this context, I am particularly grateful to the leaders of the police and the Gendarmerie, in particular the Director-General for Public Security and the President of the Police of Vienna, for this way. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on the SPÖ and the Greens.]] Ladies and gentlemen! I now come to answer the urgent question. Question 1: In recent years, left-wing extremism has re-established in Austria, addressing in particular the issues of globalisation, NATO, EU, racism and xenophobia, as well as the new Austrian Federal Government and FPÖ. Although at present the moderate spectrum is decisive, part of the Austrian anarcho and autonomous scene used appropriate opportunities to live up to its existing aggressive potential. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: Listen! Listen!]] Mainly in protests against the Federal Government and FPÖ, there have been material damage, mainly through lubrication actions, targeted provocations and the like. The recent fire attacks have to be attributed to the far-left scene due to confessional letters. [[Abg. Haigermoser: Look here!]] At present, the left-wing extremist groups in Austria still do not present an acute threat to state security in terms of the number or potential for violence. Despite this, the Austrian security authorities continue to pay particular attention to this area and observe the future development closely. On question 2: Yes. In 1999 16 material damage, of which 15 lubrication actions and a stone's throw were performed, which can be associated with the far-left scene. The height of the resulting damage is not known in detail. Body injuries have not been registered in this context. A further increase in criminal offences was observed in 2000. The exact number is not yet available. Questions 3 and 4: The most famous Austrian printing works in the far left scene are the "TATblatt" and the "Akin". As a publisher, the "Independent Initiative Information Multiple" appears in the "TATblatt" and the "Motive for Socialism" in the imprint of the "Akins". There are also a number of magazines with mainly regional significance and usually irregular publication. The issue of the financing of publications is always relevant to the security authorities in connection with investigations into criminal acts. Question 5: In compliance with the legal order for the display or prosecution of criminal acts, the security authority shall review the printing works after their publication for the incidental presence of media content offences. In the event of such suspicion, the respective edition of the printing works of the competent public prosecutor's office shall be transmitted for criminal assessment. The result of this assessment is not an inconsistency to the Federal Minister for the Interior. Questions 6 and 7: There are currently no national statistics available over the year 2000, but a special focus has been placed by the security authorities on around 1 100 events in the form of protective or regulatory measures. That's a lot!]] Question 8: In the event of demonstrations, police tactics have been and will be adapted to the situation and taken in line with the relevant level of information. If we had had such a uniform consensus yesterday in the debate on the "KommAustria", which I think would have been quite desirable, then I would not be there now to justify the request for a deadline. This request for time-limitation is filed because urgent completion of licence applications by private broadcasting operators is necessary. I would remind you that last year the Constitutional Court abolished the licences of many private broadcasting operators because of constitutional illegality. The grounds for the illegality of the Constitution were that a collegial authority with judicial intervention as the first instance of the licence authority is per se unconstitutional. The Constitutional Court considers that it would be immanent for the Constitution to create a licensing authority which is subject to the obligation of a ministry and thus opens up Parliament's control. Only under the condition of a constitutional "CommAustralia" could the establishment of a constitutional licensing authority be avoided. Ladies and gentlemen! The purpose of the application is to provide the Constitutional Committee with a period of up to 28 years. To grant or set up February 2001 within which to negotiate the matter and then pass it on to the plenary session for decision-making. Why the urgency? – Following the annulment of the decisions by the Constitutional Court, so-called provisional licences were issued. ==References====External links== On 6 December 2000, the Commission issued provisional notices in favour of the existing licensees, which are limited to six months. ==References====External links== June off. I believe that the actual sources of errors must be sought just in the time before the contract is awarded. I believe it is also important that we not only eliminate the mistakes, but that we are already trying to detect and eliminate these sources of error. Three points are essential to me at this stage: first, careful planning – planning must also be accompanied by control – second, an accurate and transparent performance description as the basis for the tender and thirdly a comprehensive and predictable soil analysis, which is important as a basis. Even in planning, it must be avoided that the future is already being addressed and bidders are preferred by certain planning services. A careful and precisely specified planning and performance description further prevents the scope for interpretation of those concerned, which usually leads to excessive demands and legal disputes. These are, of course, just a few points that I would like to make. Overall, however, I believe that this problem can be finally elucidated, even if we also extend and privatise in this area, building and road construction. This shift to the private economy allows these events to be stopped in the run-up to calls for tenders and can also at least prevent corruption in the public sector. If we privatize this area, then the public authorities will be users and tenants and no more builders. It will then be important that the rental contracts are precisely developed and that the equipment descriptions are also precisely defined. I believe that it is important to privatise and outsource this area too: where this business belongs, namely to the private sector.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e20547	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Mrs. State Secretary! High house! The total volume of public sector grants – i.e. federal government, countries, municipalities, social security funds, chambers and the like – amounts to around 300 billion schilling each year. This is just over 10 percent of the gross domestic product. In the present case, the Court of Auditors has reviewed a period of two years, namely from 1994 to 1996, in the light of selected sections of road construction. From the volume this accounts for around 5 billion schilling. Of these 5 billion schillings, 40 percent have been selected in the form of a sampling technique, so that in the end 2 billion shillings remain, which have been effectively examined by the Court of Auditors, the results of which have been submitted to the National Council in this report. With this 40 percent, which is about 2 billion, the Court of Auditors has found that there have been shortcomings that have resulted in around 50 million schilling of disadvantages to the public. Now, if you count these 50 million, this 40 percent of 5 billion, on the 5 billion schilling, then you get to the point that the damage can easily be determined and calculated with about 120 million schilling. I do not want to add to the 300 billion schilling that is awarded annually by the public authorities in Austria. If one also takes into account that no less than 80 percent of the awards examined by the Court of Auditors in road construction and 70 percent in the building construction sector were defective, this should give cause for thought. In particular, shortcomings in the tendering procedures, but also in the selection of bidders and bidders, were particularly common. About 50 percent of all cases examined by the Court of Auditors showed shortcomings in the offer review and in the bidder selection. These are exclusively errors of the issuing bodies, particularly in connection with the planning and production of the service directories. And these errors are the starting point for the speculative prices, which can then be either agreed upon by the companies offering them or in any other way be brought closer to the provider. These errors of the issuing bodies are the starting point for the financial disadvantages for the public sector, and this is ultimately none other than the taxpayer. In the Court's view, therefore, it is urgent to adopt preventive measures to prevent such deficiencies, preventive measures which must be imposed on the awarding bodies. If – and the Court of Auditors has repeatedly noted this – there is a breach of contract rules, then the great danger is that the result of the project in question will be much more expensive than originally adopted. It is therefore essential to ensure strict compliance with the Court of Auditors’ procurement rules, and in the same way also the strict control of the awarding bodies, whether by means of construction supervision, whether by means of accompanying control. Without appropriate control facilities, compliance with the procurement rules and compliance with the otherwise associated rules will not be possible on their own. Compliance with the procurement rules in conjunction with the relevant control bodies is essential for the Court of Auditors! On the one hand, this can prevent excessive prices, on the other hand – which is of course particularly common in construction – can also reduce corruption, and in the same way possible bidder agreements can be prevented. Finally, the procurement rules also serve to reduce the risk potential towards corruption. And as for corruption in particular, I should like to remind you - it has already been referred to by some of my previous speakers - that two years ago the Court of Auditors prepared a report on the fight against corruption in procurement. It has been a report that has been prepared together with the Ministry of Finance, the Ministry of Economic Affairs and the Ministry of Justice, and the position of the working group has been very clearly expressed, that it is essential that it is first of all necessary to ensure that everything goes right in the awarding bodies, because if this succeeds, then the risk potentials that come from the contractors are much lower. If the awarding bodies succeed in complying strictly with the procurement rules, much would have already been achieved. In this report from 1999 the Court of Auditors has brought in its entire wealth of experience for decades. And as far as this can be seen, this report has also fallen on very fertile soil. We have all praised him, and it would be time to implement the suggestions made by the Court of Auditors in this report. [[It was in favour of the Greens as well as of representatives of SPÖ and ÖVP.]] For example, I should like to remind you of a few new thoughts contained in this report, such as a clear legal regulation on the blockage of orders for unreliable construction operations. It's still sick of that these days. There are no clear rules in this respect, but there would be a need for such regulations. In this context, a clear rating for construction companies should also be possible. In other words, if construction operations relating to calls for tenders and the implementation of projects are to be blamed, they should be able to be reduced on the scale until finally to the total block of orders. In this context, there is a need for a legislative amendment, which is quite clear to the Court of Auditors, and I should like to recall this recommendation in this House, which is responsible for such laws. However, regardless of such legislative changes, it is appropriate to ensure that the existing rules are complied with at least by the audited bodies, by the Federal Offices, by the countries, by the municipalities and by the other bodies subject to the audit competence of the Court of Auditors. In this context, I should also like to appeal to these bodies to comply with the procurement rules. And I should like to appeal to all the departments of the Federal Government, the Land and the other local authorities, not only to those we have examined in the specific case, because this can clearly only be a small section. In this context, I should like to remind both the House and the bodies responsible for it of the way in which tax money is handled. In this context, we are talking about billions of shilling of taxpayers.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e21859	Mr. Minister! Mr President. High house! In this we differ widely, Mrs Stoisits: I believe that young people or young adults in particular already need a framework where they can focus on how to behave in society. You want to eliminate this framework. [[Congratulations on MEPs of the ÖVP.]] However, I would like to put forward a motion for an amendment by Mr Ofner, Mr Fekter and Mr Colleagues to the Jugendgerichtsgesetz. As this request is distributed, I can limit myself to the presentation of the cornerstones. In the field of the Jugendgerichtsgesetz, it is first of all a clarification as to the jurisdiction of the jury courts in criminal proceedings and in criminal proceedings against young adults, and also an improvement in the recovery of trust in the hearing of young people and, finally, a clarification of the area of responsibility of the Jugendgerichts. The law of the Court of Justice aims to clarify that the criminal proceedings against young adults are to be led by the youth departments in order to be able to use the special suitability of youth judges for this category of persons. Finally, because of the application of some of the provisions of the Jugendgerichtsgesetz, the provisions on in-force and transitional provisions must also be adapted to young adults. Let me now turn to the content of this novel. I would like to say in advance that Mr Jarolim has insidiously spoken of the great will of consensus, which has come to an end, and he would have liked to have a consensus. – In the matter itself, he did not make a single proposal that would have led us to a consensus, he did not objectly criticise the law in any matter. That is why I cannot imagine what we should have done to obtain the opposition's approval. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] This novelle has been very intensively advised – both previous speakers have mentioned this. The Permanent Parliamentary Committee on Criminal Law surveying has discussed this subject in depth at expert level, and the experts have also contributed to this innovation. This is evident in the Committee's report 404 of the supplements, in which the "absolute right" is still mentioned – already put in parentheses. However, the experts considered that, in place of an extraordinary right of mitigation, as provided for in the proposal for an initiative, the removal of the lower limits constitutes a better solution to individual case law. We have followed this proposal: For perpetrators up to 21 years old, the penalty limits are removed. I do not wish to mention that, and I strongly disagree with my previous speaker, I have been very skeptical of this solution, but have considered a unanimous expert opinion to be worthy of consideration, and that is why this provision is now contained in this decision-making novel. My scepticism is based on increasing youth crime in the area of crime and violence. In this regard, it is very well to be questioned whether the particular kindness towards young people, which Mrs Stoisits calls for, has not forced this development, namely the rise of crime. There is no doubt that the Jugendgerichtsgesetz, which has already been in force for more than ten years, is a success recipe for the return of the troubled young people and is recognised as such internationally. However, in the prevention, in deterrence of young people from crimes, this particular mildness has resulted under the slogan, "no criminalization, so that the further path is not built", the opposite, namely a massive increase in crime in drug offences and in theft, since precisely these crimes have not had any further serious consequences. However, for me and for science, a criminal law has the task of holding back potential perpetrators. Because of the expected consequences, fines, non-profit work and perpetrator-offer-compensation will be used in the future to make clear to the offenders their normal conduct. [[Calls of the Abg. Heinisch-Hosek.]] If we do not now standardise a penalty limit, I appeal to the judiciary not to ignore the preventive nature of a fine imposed on both the perpetrator himself and future perpetrators – unfortunately, this has happened several times in the case of the convictions of young people. Unfortunately, this idea, which I have explained here – namely, to question the mildness of the punishment very probably also in terms of prevention – has not been discussed sufficiently under the subtle accusation "Law and order", although the study by Beclin and Grafl, which we have discussed in the Committee of Inquiry, clearly proves that the inconsistency of young perpetrators has been dramatically overpowered and the willingness to violence of young people is increasing. Heinisch-Hosek.]] We consider that there is no need for our own pre-eminent law, i.e. a third criminal law in addition to the Youth Criminal Law and the Criminal Code, because we also - and even for adults - have the disversion and are thus far ahead of us in international comparison and have taken an exemplary course. In addition, we have included several provisions for up to 21-year-olds in the Criminal Code. The fact that we have had the extrajudicial compensation for adults for a year is likely to have gone away from you. With the regulations we are standardizing here, we can react adequately to youth sins. Youth sins no longer need to end with a judicial, criminal conviction, for which we have the division. But for further crimes – and these are no longer small criminals, but they are rather the "more serious brothers" – adult criminal law must be applied with the corresponding adjustments for youth execution. I hope that my current scepticism towards overly mild crimes with criminals will be eroded by the fact that we will see a decline in the crime of young people in the future. Thank you.

The 20- to 21-year-olds can now expect a longer penalty delay if they want to complete their vocational training. In other words, there are more privileges for groups up to the end of the 21st century. Life, and there is the possibility of greater flexibility. Please, ladies and gentlemen, in particular from the opposition, address these factual arguments in the further debate! I would like to continue to comment on this. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2001-02-01-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00056_d7e22963	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! The request for the revision of the § 64 of the Criminal Code, according to which the offences listed in paragraph 1(4) are also added to the criminal acts under the Prohibition Act, has unfortunately not found a majority in the judicial committee. I cannot accept the argument put forward in this context by representatives of the government coalition, namely the argument that one should not interfere in the affairs of another State or that it should be left to the State concerned, which is being punished on its territory. Whoever thus argues completely explores the meaning of § 64, where it is about criminal acts abroad, which are suspected without regard to the laws of the crime scene. It is a conscious exception from the territorial principle, and it is a political decision of the Austrian legislator, where he sets these exceptions, in particular for Austrian citizens. Let us remember, ladies and gentlemen: A few years ago, in the fight against child sex tourism, we introduced point 4a in § 64, according to which severe sexual abuse of unmistakable and pornographic representation with unmistakables can be suspected regardless of the criminal laws of the crime scene, if the perpetrator is Austrian or has his usual residence in the country. At that time, there was no one in my knowledge who opposed interference in the powers of persecution of another state. Rather, in this House, everyone rightly felt that child sex tourism and the associated criminal acts against unmistakables are so bad that they must at least be punished under the given conditions. It is similar to my view in criminal acts according to the Prohibition Act. It should not be that Austrians, for example, act in Germany within the meaning of the Prohibition Act. We know that the German legal situation in this context is less stringent than the Austrian one. While it is true that the actual application cases for such a new criminal provision – thank God, I have to say – would be rather rare, but a close gap would be closed. Consideration should also be given to the symbolic and political effect that the Austrian legislator would bring about with such a law. In this context, I would also like to point out that the current debate in Germany on the prohibition of the NPD and the application to the Federal Constitutional Court in this regard gives rise to an increased sensitivity to this issue. In order to leave the church in the village, Mr Federal Minister, I do not want to derive from the present proposal for a law a large number of principles. I believe that the political reasons I have put forward are in favour of accepting this proposal for a law, but I regret that the government coalition did not want to compromise. – In this sense: Thank you.

Originally, the legal regulation was that a new pharmacy, if it wanted to open, had to have a supply potential of 5,500 persons to be supplied. At the same time, another pharmacy was not allowed to fall under this supply status. The Constitutional Court has repealed parts of these provisions, namely those provisions which provide that a pharmacy must prove at least 5 500 persons to be supplied. As a result, there has been a "threat" – under quotation marks – of the medical house pharmacies, and in 1998, Mrs. Hostasch created a transitional scheme for ten years, after which the medical house pharmacies may remain in any case during this period. At the time, I went to the speaker's desk and I had Mrs Hostasch's prophecy – that is to say, I have the protocol here – that this regulation of a review by the Constitutional Court will not stand. The Constitutional Court has immediately initiated an examination procedure, has taken a decision and will certainly not take the constitutional concerns into account in the individual consideration or treatment. The background is not a political spicy cap, but a serious attempt to bring light into this dark, which animal welfare organisations have shown: in one and a half years of research work, with a lot of hidden investigation, with countless volunteer staff equipped with cameras, with cameras, who volunteered to do a job that would actually have been the work of the health resort, the veterinary supervision, the supervision of the Ministry of Agriculture. [[Unrest in the hall.]] [[President Dr. Fischer gives the bell sign.]] Thank you, Mr President. In reality, this would not have been the work of NGOs, but the work of political responsibility and political control. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. Arbitrator.]] As far as investigation committees are concerned, the advantage is that the opposition through control can also improve and optimise the work of a government, so far not in the minds of the government groups. That control in the sense of the controlled can be that it can overall improve the level of transparency and openness in a political system has not yet penetrated into the minds of the government groups in that country. I think this is a very good, excellent occasion now, because responsibility is really wide-ranging through the political spectrum. This would be a first occasion for investigating political responsibility in the question of knowingly to tolerate health risks for consumers, knowingly to tolerate environmental hazards and knowingly cover systems over at least 10, 15 or 20 years. I would ask you to take this into account if you are going to blaze easily now and will probably reject the request. I would ask you once again to consider what the criteria for you as a government group are for accepting a request for committee of inquiry! If there are criteria such as these: widespread uncertainty of a large population group, widespread illegal networking practices with neighbouring countries, so that interpol, border guards, customs traps and so on – what if not this case, which is worth an investigation committee? [[Congratulation with the greens.]] You really have to explain to me how far it has to come in Austria so that you can agree to a request for an investigation committee! One last thing maybe: I have already given you the quotations this morning about how old this scandal is. It goes back to 1984. Since this year, these newspaper reports and the indications of veterinary, dedicated veterinarians, environmental and animal welfare organisations have been available. They have been driving consumers around the nose for 15 years and simply silenced a system that has a massive negative impact on health and the environment and also on the confidence of consumers in this country. I mean, if you do not agree with this committee of inquiry, there are hardly any further stages of increase, except for political inquisition. If you see political investigation committees exclusively as a disciplining element against the opposition and not as an instrument for clarifying political responsibility, then today you are probably voting against it and perhaps again. But then you have lost for me any credibility as control parties or as parties interested in any way in the investigation of political abuses in that country. Thank you.
